📑 The Encumbrance Certificate in Telangana: Form 15, Form 16, and what neither shows

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Encumbrance Certificate Telangana Land Buying Guide 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • The EC lists every REGISTERED transaction on a property — sales, mortgages, gifts, leases, partitions, attachments — from the Sub-Registrar’s records.
  • Form 15 = transactions exist (each with parties, numbers, deed type); Form 16 = Nil EC — and a Nil on ancestral land may just mean the family never registered anything.
  • IGRS digital records start January 1983; earlier history lives in the SRO’s physical Index Books.
  • Agricultural land: Bhu Bharati holds the recent records (2018+) — check BOTH portals for converted parcels.
  • An MRO-level charge in the Pahani’s liabilities column may never appear in any EC — read the two together.
  • Certified fees: ₹200 up to 30 years, ₹500 beyond; manual SRO searches take 15–30 days.

What the EC is — and its two blind spots

The Registration department’s statement of every registered transaction over a chosen period: reading the chain top to bottom shows how the land travelled to the present seller and what still sits charged on it. Blind spot one: TIME — digital records begin 1 Jan 1983; a seller who bought in the 1970s and held quietly has a clean online EC that says nothing about the early years; manual Index-Book search at the SRO is the only complete record for older parcels. Blind spot two: REGISTRATION — unregistered hand loans, pending litigation, unregistered agreements and MRO-level charges never appear; that is why the EC is read ALONGSIDE the Pahani, never instead of it.

Getting it online

1) The right portal: urban/non-agricultural → registration.telangana.gov.in (Citizen login); agricultural → bhubharati.telangana.gov.in under Land Details Search; recently converted parcels → BOTH. 2) Online Services → Encumbrance Search (EC) → accept the disclaimer → search by document number (most precise) or property details (district, SRO, village, survey number). 3) Set the period: 30 YEARS for a purchase — short windows miss disputes that settled years ago but still shape the chain. 4) Form 15 or Form 16 appears; the free view is fine for research; for a bank file pay ₹200/₹500 and download the signed PDF IMMEDIATELY — the system does not always allow re-downloads without re-paying.

Reading the entries

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
Document number + yearEach registered transaction’s IDCross-check against the seller’s stated history; unexplained entries need explanations
Deed typeSale, mortgage, gift, partition, release, leaseAn ACTIVE mortgage = debt on the parcel — cleared before your clean title
PartiesNames in each transactionEvery name must connect to the next transaction — a non-connecting name is a break in title
ConsiderationDeclared valueOddly low old values sometimes hide real consideration — check context
SRO detailsWhere each deed registeredMust match the property’s jurisdiction; cross-SRO deals on one parcel are a red flag
StatusForm 15 vs Form 16Form 16 on old/ancestral parcels may be a FALSE negative — combine with manual checks

Good sign: Form 15 with a clean chain ending at the current seller, no live mortgage, every transaction closing the previous one, one consistent SRO.

The traps that cost real money

Nil EC on quietly-inherited land — no registrations ≠ no claims: pair with the Pahani, possession enquiry and heir checks. The loan that never shows — deposit-of-deeds and MRO-recorded charges bypass the register: demand a bank no-dues letter and read the Pahani liabilities column. Chain breaks — a gap between transfers means an unregistered link document: trace it before proceeding; lenders will refuse the gap anyway. Pre-1983 assumed clean — never buy an older parcel on the online EC alone.

Example: The 30-year discipline in one line: a 5-year EC can look spotless while a 12-year-old mortgage sits alive just outside the window. The default search period is set for convenience, not for your protection — always override it.

Quick answers (FAQ)

What period should I search? 30 years for any purchase. Is Form 16 good news? Only with corroboration. Is the online EC valid for banks? Yes — the signed digital certificate is accepted; banks re-verify the chain themselves. EC or Pahani? Both — registered charges vs possession/classification/revenue-level liabilities.

Bottom line

The EC answers one question with authority — what was REGISTERED — and stays silent on everything else. Thirty years, both portals for converted land, the Pahani beside it, and manual books for the pre-1983 years: that is a complete encumbrance check.

For you: Your standard EC order for any parcel: 30-year certified pull by YOU (never the seller’s copy), same-day Pahani liabilities read, and for pre-1983 chains the manual SRO search budgeted into the deal timeline. On your ventures, include the 30-year Form 15 in each buyer’s file — it pre-empts the bank’s slowest query. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 EC on IGRS: the right portal, the right period, the right reading

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Encumbrance Certificate Telangana: Check EC Online on IGRS — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • In Telangana the EC issues through IGRS at registration.telangana.gov.in — NOT through the Bhu Bharati land-records module; searching the wrong portal is the #1 confusion on the new system.
  • Every registered sale, mortgage, gift, release and lien appears as an entry — parties, date, document number.
  • The EC reflects registered documents ONLY: unregistered hand loans, pending litigation and unregistered agreements never appear.
  • A 5-year EC can look clean while a 12-year-old mortgage sits outside the window — pull 30 years for purchases.
  • Banks sanction against the EC chain, not the seller’s word: no release entry, no sanction.

Where it lives, and why that matters

Two systems, two jobs: Bhu Bharati holds the land RECORD (Pahani, mutation, Bhudhaar); IGRS holds the REGISTRATION side (EC, market value, deed copies). If you are hunting the EC inside Bhu Bharati you are on the wrong portal. Reading an EC chain top to bottom shows how the land travelled to the present seller — and what still sits charged on it.

Pulling it, step by step

1) registration.telangana.gov.in → Encumbrance Search. 2) Pick the search key: document number + year if you have a prior deed (most precise); otherwise survey number or house number. 3) Set the period — 30 years for a purchase decision, never the default few. 4) Pay the statutory fee, download, SAVE the PDF — lenders will ask for exactly this.

Entry-type literacy

EntryWhat it isWhat it means for you
Sale deedOwnership transferBuilds the chain to the present seller
MortgageLoan charged on the landMust be RELEASED before or at your purchase
Release / dischargeLoan closedConfirms an earlier mortgage cleared
Gift / settlementFamily transferCheck stamping and acceptance were proper
Agreement of sale (regd.)A prior committed saleA LIVE agreement can block your purchase
Lien / attachmentCourt or department chargeSTOP until formally lifted

Good sign: a continuous chain old owner → present seller, every mortgage matched by a later release, no live agreement or attachment.

The four casual-reader traps

Nil treated as clean — nil means no registered entries IN THE WINDOW: widen to 30 years and still verify Pahani and possession. The missing loan — unregistered borrowings never appear: bank no-dues letter + Pahani liabilities column. Wrong property searched — survey-number typos and door-number confusion pull a different parcel’s EC: cross-check the EC’s schedule against the deed and LPM. Break in the chain — a gap suggests an unregistered link document: trace it first; lenders refuse gaps anyway.

Why lenders read it first

Sanction runs on the chain: mortgage without release = stop. Reading the EC the same way BEFORE your advance puts you ahead of the problem instead of behind it. Pair it always with the Pahani — EC for registered charges, Pahani for possession and classification; the two together are the minimum diligence for any Telangana purchase.

Example: Red flag: a seller offering an EC for a conveniently short period, or discouraging the 30-year search, is steering you around an old entry. Pull the full period yourself — it is your ₹200, and your protection.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Where do I get the EC? IGRS — registration.telangana.gov.in, Encumbrance Search. Search without a document number? Yes — survey or house number; document-number is sharper. Valid for banks? Yes — the digital certificate is accepted; banks re-verify. EC or Pahani — which matters more? Both, together, always.

Bottom line

Right portal (IGRS), right key (document number), right period (30 years), right reading (every mortgage needs its release) — the EC is only as protective as the search you configure.

For you: Teach your whole team the two-portal split — record on Bhu Bharati, registration on IGRS — because half of “the portal shows nothing” panics are wrong-portal searches. And keep a one-line entry glossary (this lesson’s table) pinned in your office: entry-type literacy is what separates reading an EC from merely downloading one. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 The 22A prohibited-land check: two portals, one clause, dated screenshots

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: 22A Prohibited Land Telangana Check Guide 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • Section 22A of the Registration Act lets the state BAR parcels from registration entirely — once listed, the Sub-Registrar must refuse any sale, gift or mortgage deed. No discretion, no workaround.
  • Six categories: government land, Endowment/Waqf, ceiling surplus, assigned land, court-disputed parcels, and land notified as against public policy.
  • The 2017-18 records updation and the 2020 Dharani rollout WRONGLY flagged thousands of genuine private patta parcels; an Aug 2025 high-level committee is reviewing a list covering over ONE CRORE acres.
  • The list updates continuously and not in real time on every device — check the DAY you decide, and again the morning of registration.
  • The Telangana HC requires inclusions to follow Rules 238–241 (2016 Registration Rules) and to cite the specific clause — improper listings are challengeable.

What 22A is

The state’s registration firewall: survey numbers on the prohibited list simply cannot register. It protects public assets in theory; in practice the list’s errors create TWO buyer risks — the obvious one (you try to register, the SRO blocks, your money is stuck) and the subtle one (the parcel looked clean on an old check, and the 22A flag surfaces at the counter because the portal was not refreshed).

Definition — The clause ladder: 22A-1(a) assigned land · (b) government land · (c) Endowment/Waqf · (d) ceiling surplus · (e) court-ordered restriction · (f) government-notified prohibition. Each carries different weight — (a) cannot be sold without government regularisation; (e) can dissolve when the case does. Knowing WHICH clause tells you whether resolution is even possible.

Running the check (free, two minutes)

1) Agricultural: bhubharati.telangana.gov.in → Land Details Search → district, mandal, village, survey number → read the “Prohibited Status” column. 2) Urban/non-agricultural: registration.telangana.gov.in → “Prohibited Properties” → same details; a red indicator or a clause like “Under 22A-1(a)” means blocked at the SRO. 3) Read the SPECIFIC clause, not just the status — it decides whether there is any path forward. 4) SCREENSHOT with the date visible — if the status changes between your check and registration day, you hold documented proof of what it showed when you decided. Always check BOTH portals THE SAME DAY: survey numbers clean on one have appeared prohibited on the other in known cases.

What the result shows

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
Survey numberThe parcel checkedExactly matches the parcel shown on the GROUND
Prohibited statusBlocked from registration or notAny flag = SRO refusal; no exceptions without a delisting order
22A clauseWhich sub-clause appliesDecides removability — (a) needs regularisation; (e) tracks the court case
Reason descriptionWhy it is listedCross-check the seller’s explanation; inconsistency is a serious warning
District / mandal / villageLocation of the entryMatch the physical location, not just the seller’s paperwork

The offline route, and fighting a wrong listing

At the SRO or Tahsildar: ask for the prohibited-register entry for your survey number — under HC guidelines it must show the DATE of inclusion and the clause; an office that cannot produce them may be sitting on a procedurally challengeable listing. Genuine patta land wrongly listed: file a grievance through Bhu Bharati’s TM 15 module (inclusion-in-prohibited-list grievances) with title deeds and Passbook attached — the Collector is the competent authority for removal under most clauses; if Revenue fails to act within 90 days, the High Court can be approached for a writ of mandamus.

The timing trap

Properties have been ADDED to the list between a buyer’s first check and registration day — blocking the transaction after money had moved. The discipline: check on the day you finalise the price, and again the morning you go to the SRO. Dates matter; screenshots prove them.

Example: For perspective: with over one crore acres on a list under active review, the 22A check is not paranoia about rare fraud — it is hygiene on a system the government itself admits needs auditing. Two minutes, two portals, one dated screenshot.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Can 22A land ever be bought? Only after formal delisting — clause-dependent; assigned land effectively never. My genuine patta land is listed — what now? TM 15 grievance with proof → Collector → writ after 90 days of inaction. Which portal? Both, same day: Bhu Bharati for agricultural, IGRS for urban. Is a clean check permanent? No — recheck at every decision point.

Bottom line

One list decides whether your deed can even be presented. Check both portals on decision day, read the clause, screenshot the date — and treat every listed parcel as unbuyable until a delisting order says otherwise.

For you: You already run 22A as gate one — upgrade it to the full protocol: BOTH portals, same-day, clause noted, dated screenshot into the parcel folder, and a re-check the morning of every registration in your ventures. For wrongly-listed landowner partners, knowing the TM 15 → Collector → writ ladder makes you the developer who can actually unlock their stuck parcel — a sourcing edge nobody else in the room has. 📑 The 22A list on IGRS: category by category, cure by cure

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: 22A Prohibited Property Telangana: Check the List First 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • Section 22A lets the state notify land whose sale documents the sub-registrar MUST refuse — maintained as a searchable list of survey numbers on IGRS.
  • Categories: government, assigned, endowment, wakf and court-attached land — each fails a purchase for a DIFFERENT reason.
  • Prohibited parcels look normal on the ground and often carry plausible-looking paper — the LIST, not the seller’s file, is the authority.
  • PARTIAL listings exist: one subdivision prohibited, the rest free — so the check runs on the exact survey number and extent.
  • A 22A listing is not a complication to manage — it is a BAR. Stop until the entry is formally deleted.

Why each category fails differently

Government land was never the seller’s to sell. Assigned land was granted to the landless with restrictions that persist for decades. Endowment and wakf property belongs to institutions whose alienation needs statutory sanction, rarely granted. Attached land is frozen by a court until told otherwise. The buyer’s trap is uniform though: on the ground they all look like ordinary parcels with ordinary fences.

Running the check on IGRS

Free, no login — the single highest-value two minutes in Telangana diligence. 1) registration.telangana.gov.in → prohibited property search (a REGISTRATION-department list). 2) Drill to district, mandal, village — and confirm the REVENUE village; boundaries shifted in places. 3) Search the exact survey number AND its subdivisions where they exist — partial prohibitions attach to subdivisions. 4) If listed, note the CATEGORY — it decides the (rare) cure path.

Categories and cure paths

CategoryWhy it is barredAny path forward?
Government landNot the seller’s to alienateNone for a private buyer
Assigned landGranted with transfer restrictionsOnly per the assignment law’s own terms
Endowment propertyInstitutional land; sanction neededStatutory permission — rare in practice
Wakf propertyWakf Board alienation rulesBoard sanction — exceptional
Court attachedFrozen pending proceedingsOnly after the attachment lifts
Wrongly listedClerical / legacy entryDeletion via RDO / Collector with proof

Good sign: a clean search on the exact survey number and its subdivisions, saved as a dated screenshot beside your EC and Pahani.

The four complications, none of which excuse skipping it

Wrongly listed parcel — legacy and clerical entries occur: the OWNER applies for deletion via RDO/Collector with ownership proof; you buy only after formal removal. Partial listing — a subdivision is barred, the rest free: get the exact extent and subdivision mapped by survey records first. “Deletion is imminent” — promises substitute for nothing: wait until the entry actually vanishes, and recheck yourself. Assigned land offered cheap — the classic void deal: restrictions survive the sale and the money rarely comes back. Walk.

Why 22A precedes the EC

An EC tells you about charges on a SALEABLE parcel; the 22A list tells you whether the parcel is saleable AT ALL. Run it first — no clean EC rescues a prohibited survey number. And recheck near registration: lists update, and the gap between first search and SRO appointment is exactly where surprises land.

Example: Red flag: a seller pushing urgency on a bargain-priced parcel while discouraging a free two-minute list check is telling you, indirectly, what the check would say. Believe them.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Does a clean EC override a listing? Never — the EC reports charges; 22A bars registration entirely. Can assigned land be bought? Only within the assignment law’s narrow terms. Wrongly listed? Owner pursues deletion; you wait for the record, not the promise. Recheck before registration? Always — dated screenshots of both checks.

Bottom line

Order of operations is the lesson: 22A first (is it saleable?), EC second (is it clean?), everything else after. Exact survey number, subdivisions included, dated screenshot, recheck at the SRO date.

For you: Pair this with the deep-dive assigned-land and 22A-check classes in the school — together they are your acquisition firewall. Process upgrade: your parcel folder’s first page should be the two dated 22A screenshots (decision day + registration morning). When a lender or JV partner audits your venture, that page answers their first question before they ask it. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 Registration slot booking: where all your diligence gets consumed

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Registration Slot Booking in Telangana: Bhu Bharati Guide 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • Slot booking is the appointment system for executing registration — agricultural land books inside Bhu Bharati and REQUIRES the parcel’s verified permanent Bhudhaar; non-agricultural books through IGRS.
  • The Bhudhaar precondition is the anti-fraud spine: no verified parcel identity, no slot — which blocks the old double-registration scripts.
  • Stamp duty is paid by challan BEFORE the appointment, on the higher of government value and deal price; the challan number joins the booking.
  • Parties, not just papers, must attend: executants, claimants, witnesses, with ID — a missing party is the top reason a confirmed slot produces no registration.
  • Book only after the document set is COMPLETE — slots taken on hope become reschedules.

How the system works

The slot ties the deed to a uniquely identified parcel before anyone sits at the registrar’s desk. Practical consequences: your booking problems are usually IDENTIFIER problems in disguise (a temporary Bhudhaar, a mismatched record); and the money must be sequenced correctly — duty computed on the higher-of rule, paid by challan, amounts matching, before the appointment exists.

Booking, step by step

1) Confirm the Bhudhaar: verified PERMANENT status on the record — temporary means fix that first; everything else waits. 2) Compute and pay the challan on the higher of government value and deal price; keep the challan number with the deed draft. 3) Book: office, date, time; enter parties, parcel and challan details — choosing a slot ALL parties can actually attend. 4) Attend with the full set at the booked hour — late arrival can forfeit the slot on busy days.

What to carry

ItemWho needs itNotes
Sale deed (final)All partiesPrinted per drafting rules; unsigned until told
Challan proofBuyer typicallyAmount must match the duty computation
Aadhaar / IDEvery party and witnessNames matching the DEED spelling
Pattadar PassbookSellerLinks the parcel to the record
PhotographsPartiesPer current office requirements
PAN where applicablePartiesHigh-value transactions require it

Good sign: a slot booked after EC, 22A and Bhudhaar all cleared, a challan matching the duty maths, every party present with ID at the hour.

The four failure patterns

Slot will not open — temporary/unverified Bhudhaar: resolve on the record; the slot follows. Challan mismatch — paid duty ≠ computed base: recompute on higher-of and regularise before the date. A party cannot attend — one missing executant voids the appointment: reschedule EARLY per portal rules, don’t forfeit on the day. Documents fail scrutiny — name mismatches, stale EC: correct the record, pull fresh checks, rebook clean.

The exam-day framing

Everything earlier converges here: Pahani, EC, 22A, market value, Bhudhaar all get CONSUMED at this appointment. Buyers who did the sequence sail through; buyers who skipped steps discover it at the registrar’s desk with money already committed. Treat the appointment as the exam and the checks as the syllabus — nothing new should be learned at the SRO, because anything learned there is learned with your money on the table.

Example: Red flag: an agent promising a same-day slot on a parcel whose Bhudhaar is still temporary is promising to bypass the one control that protects you. Whatever the method — decline it.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Why does the slot need a Bhudhaar? So the deed binds to a uniquely identified parcel — the anti-double-registration control. Where do I book? Agricultural: Bhu Bharati; non-agricultural: IGRS. When is duty paid? Before the appointment, by challan, higher-of rule. Can I reschedule? Yes, within portal rules — early beats forfeiting. What kills appointments? Missing parties, challan mismatches, unverified Bhudhaar, record-name discrepancies — every one checkable the week before.

Bottom line

The slot is a gate that only opens on completed homework. Book late in your process, not early in your optimism.

For you: Run registrations like launches: a T-minus-7 checklist (Bhudhaar permanent ✓, challan matched ✓, all signatories’ availability confirmed ✓, fresh EC + 22A ✓) for every SRO date in your ventures. Multiple plot registrations a week means every avoidable reschedule is direct revenue slippage. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 Government market value by survey number: the state’s price beneath your price

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Land Market Value in Telangana: Check by Survey Number — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • The government “market value” (basic value) is the per-unit rate for each survey number/locality, maintained by the registration department on IGRS.
  • Duty and fees compute on it — or on the deal price if HIGHER. Under-declaring saves nothing: duty still charges on the government value, plus scrutiny.
  • Units differ: agricultural per ACRE, non-agricultural per SQUARE YARD — and adjacent survey numbers can carry sharply different rates (highway-touch vs behind it).
  • It lives on IGRS, not Bhu Bharati — the most common dead-end search on the new system.
  • Pull the rate EARLY: duty and fees are a meaningful percentage, and lenders expect you to know the number.

What the value is

The state’s floor price for stamp-duty purposes, survey number by survey number. The higher-of rule is what buyers miss: declare below the government value and duty is still charged on the government value; declare above and duty follows your price. There is no arbitrage in under-declaration — only legal exposure. (This is Telangana’s version of the guideline-value concept covered in the school’s tax classes.)

Checking it (free, two minutes)

1) registration.telangana.gov.in → Market Value Search — the registration department’s service. 2) Choose the LAND TYPE first: agricultural (per acre) vs non-agricultural (per square yard) — pick wrong and the number misleads you. 3) District → mandal → village → survey number; adjacent surveys can differ sharply. 4) Note the unit rate, multiply by your extent, keep the figure for duty maths — and compare against your negotiated price; the higher prevails.

Where the value bites

StageHow it appliesWhat to do
BudgetingDuty + fees computed on itAdd them to the deal price early
NegotiationA large gap either way needs explainingAsk WHY the price sits far from the rate
RegistrationSRO applies higher-ofCarry your computation; verify the challan
Bank loanValuers cross-check both figuresExpect questions on big divergence
Resale laterCapital-gains reference valuesKeep the rate record from purchase day

Good sign: a deal price sitting explainably near the government rate, duty computed on the correct base, a challan matching your own maths.

The four value-check mistakes

Wrong land type selected — agricultural rates read against a non-agricultural parcel or vice versa: re-run with the classification from the Pahani. Deal far below the rate — duty charges on the government value anyway and the gap invites scrutiny: price honestly. Adjacent-survey confusion — the neighbour’s premium rate read as yours: confirm exact survey number and LPM before computing. Rate seems outdated — revisions land periodically and portals lag: verify at the SRO for a live transaction; carry a dated printout.

The negotiation read

Sellers quote prices; the state quotes values — and the DISTANCE between them is information. A price far above the rate needs justifying features (frontage, corner, road width); far below it needs an explanation that is not a title problem in disguise. Do the full arithmetic before your offer — price + duty + fees — because that total, not the sticker, is what competes with other parcels.

Example: Red flag: anyone proposing to register below the government value “to save you duty” is proposing something that does not work arithmetically and exposes you legally. Decline.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Where do I check? IGRS Market Value Search — not Bhu Bharati. Duty on price or value? Whichever is higher. Same rates for farm and urban land? No — per acre vs per square yard; the search asks first. Why is the next survey different? Rates set survey by survey — roads, use and locality lines create sharp steps. Does it affect my loan? Valuers compare both figures; unexplained divergence slows sanction.

Bottom line

Know the state’s number before you argue about the seller’s. Right land type, exact survey number, dated printout, higher-of maths — and the challan will never surprise you.

For you: Track basic-value REVISIONS across your belts like market intelligence — the state raising a village’s rate is the state telling you where it sees demand (and your buyers’ closing costs rising). And in negotiations, open with the IGRS rate printed: anchoring on the state’s number moves family sellers more than any comparable you quote. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 Tracking Bhu Bharati applications: the acknowledgment number is the case

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Bhu Bharati Application Status: Track by Number — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • Application status is the portal’s live view of any filed request — mutation, correction, certificates — tracked by acknowledgment number.
  • The number is not a receipt; it is the case’s IDENTITY. Quote it and any office finds the file; lose it and you are asking strangers to search thousands of filings by name.
  • The arc: submitted → under verification → enquiry/hearing → approved/rejected → record updated. Verification is where thin-document files sit.
  • Time-bound processing under the RoR Act means stages carry expectations — six weeks parked at verification is NOT normal anymore.
  • Correction cases race the 13 April 2026 deadline: track weekly; a late-discovered rejection may leave no room to cure and refile.

Tracking online

1) bhubharati.telangana.gov.in → application tracking (mutation, correction and certificate requests). 2) Acknowledgment number + captcha → current stage and HOLDING OFFICE — and save a screenshot each time; the screenshots build your timeline. 3) Read the stage honestly: submitted and verification are normal early; weeks without movement are not — note the office named, because that is where you follow up. 4) Act on outcomes: approved → pull the updated record and VERIFY it; rejected → get the reason and cure fast — near deadlines, refiling is a same-week task.

Escalating offline

Start at the Tahsildar: quote the number, ask what the file awaits, supply it that day if you can. Persistent non-movement goes up one level to the RDO — WITH your dated screenshots. Revenue grievance days and MeeSeva complaints create the written record of delay that escalation runs on.

The stage ladder

StatusWhat it meansYour move
SubmittedFiled, not yet picked upWait; recheck in a week
Under verificationOfficer examining documentsAsk what is missing if it lingers
Enquiry / hearingField check or appearance neededATTEND — absence lapses cases
ApprovedOrders passedPull the updated record and verify
RejectedRefused with a reasonCure the defect; refile fast
Record updatedChange reflected in PahaniDownload a fresh certified copy

Good sign: a file moving stage to stage within days, a named office you can visit, an outcome verified against the fresh record.

The four tracking failures

Number returns nothing — typo, or the filing never entered the system: recheck digits, then confirm at the filing office with your paper acknowledgment. Stuck at verification for weeks — thin documents or a parked file: visit the Tahsildar with the number; supply what is missing in person. Rejected without a clear reason — demand the specific defect IN WRITING; you cannot cure what is unnamed. Approved but record unchanged — sync lag: allow days, then raise the mismatch with the ORDER number at the MRO.

Why watching wins

A weekly check with dated screenshots turns you from a hopeful petitioner into a DOCUMENTED one — and documentation is what the RDO responds to at escalation. For buyers mid-transaction the tracker is protection: never release the final payment while the seller’s promised correction or mutation still reads “submitted”. And usefully — WITH the number, the portal shows anyone the status: buyers can verify a seller’s claimed filings before paying. That is deliberate.

Example: Red flag: an agent who says “the application is moving” but cannot produce the acknowledgment number is describing an application that may not exist. No number, no filing.

Quick answers (FAQ)

What number do I need? The acknowledgment number from filing. How long per stage? Days to a few weeks; past a month deserves escalation. Rejected — now what? Specific reason → cure → refile, same week near deadlines. Can I track someone else’s application? With the number, yes — use it to verify sellers’ claims.

Bottom line

The acknowledgment number is the case; the weekly screenshot is the strategy; and payment against a promise waits until the tracker says the promise came true.

For you: Two standing rules for your operation: an “applications register” (number, filed date, stage, next check) reviewed every Monday for everything pending across your parcels — and in purchase agreements, milestone payments tied to TRACKER STATES (“on mutation showing approved”), not to seller assurances. The portal becomes your escrow officer. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 HMDA & DTCP layout approval: the LP number, the right portal, and the mortgage-plot check

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: HMDA DTCP Layout Approval Telangana Guide 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • HMDA layout approval sanctions layouts within the Hyderabad Metropolitan Region; DTCP covers everywhere else (Telangana Urban Area Development Act 1975). Both assign the LP number that gives a layout legal standing.
  • A Gram Panchayat sanction is NOT layout approval — it carries no weight for registration or bank loans; buyers who confused the two have paid for plots they cannot register.
  • Since April 2024, new HMDA applications run on BuildNow (buildnow.telangana.gov.in); DPMS handles older files and CLU; DTCP districts use dpms.dtcp.telangana.gov.in.
  • The RIGHT portal matters: a valid LP number searched on the wrong portal returns nothing — creating false alarm or false confidence.
  • HMDA holds developer plots as MORTGAGE security during approval — some are not released for sale; check whether yours is one.

Which authority, which portal

HMDA territory: Hyderabad, Medchal, parts of Rangareddy, Sangareddy, Medak, Siddipet, Bhuvanagiri. DTCP: every other district. A seller waving HMDA approval for a plot in Nalgonda, Karimnagar or Khammam has raised the first red flag — those are DTCP territory. Portals: pre-April-2024 HMDA layouts → dpms.hmda.gov.in; post-April-2024 → buildnow.telangana.gov.in; DTCP → dpms.dtcp.telangana.gov.in. Converted zones near Hyderabad that moved between jurisdictions during boundary changes can have entries on BOTH systems — check both.

Verifying the LP number

Free and instant. 1) Ask the seller for the approval PROCEEDINGS order and the LP number with year — before any other conversation. 2) Search: DPMS public search by file number/applicant/project (filter by district, mandal, dates); BuildNow’s application tracking; DTCP portal by district + LP number. 3) Read status AND the attached conditions; download the approved layout DRAWING — exact plot boundaries, road widths, open spaces as sanctioned — and match it against the physical site. 4) The LP number must appear on the proceedings document AND the drawing, matching the portal exactly; a developer who cannot produce them, stop.

What the approval contains

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
LP numberThe layout’s legal identityOn proceedings + drawing + portal, all matching
Approved drawingSanctioned plan: plots, roads, open spacesSite must MATCH it — discrepancies mean plots sold outside the plan
Approval authorityHMDA or DTCP by locationAuthority must match the district
Developer + survey numbersApplicant and parcels coveredCross-check deed survey numbers fall INSIDE the approved extent
ConditionsRoads, open-space handover, drainageUnfulfilled conditions = approved but not legally complete for sales
Open Space ReservationAreas that cannot be sold or builtVerify YOUR plot number is not in the reserved zone

The offline route — and the mortgage check

HMDA main office: Swarna Jayanti Complex, Ameerpet (visiting hours 3–5 pm); DTCP at AC Guards or district offices. Ask for the complete layout PROCEEDINGS for the LP number — the physical order with conditions, open-space requirements and road dedications, more detailed than the status screen — and the stamped approved drawing, matching your plot number against it. Then the check most buyers never run: is your specific plot one HMDA holds as mortgage security? HMDA takes developer plots as security during approval; some are not released for sale until the mortgage is discharged. The office confirms release status — and the DPMS public search shows mortgaged plots for many layouts. Run it before ANY advance.

Example: The trap in one sentence: a genuine LP number does not make YOUR plot saleable — it can sit in the OSR zone, outside the approved extent, or under HMDA mortgage, all inside a “fully approved” layout. Verify the layout, then verify the PLOT.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Is panchayat approval enough? Never — no weight for registration or loans. Which portal for my district? HMDA belt → BuildNow/DPMS; everywhere else → DTCP’s DPMS. What if the number returns nothing? First confirm you are on the RIGHT portal; then treat as unapproved until proven otherwise. What makes a layout “complete”? LP number + conditions fulfilled + your plot inside the drawing and outside OSR/mortgage.

Bottom line

Three verifications, in order: the AUTHORITY matches the district, the LP NUMBER matches portal-proceedings-drawing, and YOUR PLOT sits clean inside the sanctioned plan — released, saleable, outside the reserved areas.

For you: Your ventures live under these regimes — so weaponise transparency: print the LP number, portal link and approved drawing into every brochure, and walk buyers through the mortgage-release status of their specific plot unprompted. In a market where “approved layout” is the most abused phrase, showing the machinery is the premium. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 HMDA Development Permission: tentative LP, final LP, and the 15% mortgage

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: HMDA Development Permission Telangana 2026: LP Number Guide — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • HMDA Development Permission is the formal sanction for layouts, group housing, multiplexes and high-rises in the metropolitan region — under the HMDA Act 2008 and TS-bPASS Act 2020.
  • Two stages: TENTATIVE layout plan (after AI scrutiny of drawings + fee), then FINAL LP after officials physically inspect roads, drainage, parks and parking on the ground.
  • Every approved layout carries a unique LP number (format LP No. 000123/HMDA/LO/YYYY) — the single anchor a buyer must check.
  • HMDA layouts must MORTGAGE 15% of plotted area with the municipality — released only after amenities are physically built and certified.
  • From late 2025 every fresh application files through BuildNow; TG-bPASS stopped accepting new submissions 15 Dec 2025. Older approvals stay valid; only the filing route changed.

What the permission is

The only document that turns raw land inside the metropolitan region into a legally developable, marketable layout. HMDA’s writ runs across seven planning zones — including Ghatkesar, Medchal, Shamshabad and Shankarpally — with the Metropolitan Commissioner signing off on the Director’s recommendation. The two-stage structure is the buyer’s friend: a TENTATIVE LP means drawings passed scrutiny; only the FINAL LP means the roads and parks physically exist as sanctioned.

Definition — Tentative vs Final LP: tentative = the plan is approved on paper; final = the District Level Committee / HMDA inspected the ground and certified it matches the drawing. Plots sell safely against FINAL status — a venture marketing hard on a tentative LP is selling a promise, not a layout.

The application flow (developer’s view — and the buyer’s map of it)

1) Register on BuildNow — separate logins for owner and the Licensed Technical Person (only HMDA-listed architects, engineers, surveyors can submit drawings); the registration name must match the sale deed EXACTLY or the file bounces at scrutiny. 2) The LTP uploads architectural and structural drawings, layout plan, soil report, rainwater-harvesting plan, EC, tax receipt, 30-year link documents and self-certification declarations — the AI engine validates against zoning, road widths and FAR in minutes. 3) The Director approves / returns / rejects; on approval the Commissioner signs and a FEE LETTER lands — development charges, scrutiny fee, betterment charges, 1% labour cess — payable within 15 days. 4) The digitally signed Proceedings and Drawings auto-issue, LP number on every page — then run that number through dpms.hmda.gov.in public search to confirm it is live. Offline still handles: mortgage deeds, infrastructure-completion proofs, the physical site inspection that moves tentative → final, and the mortgage RELEASE order (office at Swarna Jayanti Complex, Ameerpet, 3–5 pm).

Reading a DP, field by field

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
LP numberThe layout’s permit IDLoads on the DPMS public search — instantly
File numberInternal application referenceCross-check on Online DPMS
Survey numbers + villageParcels coveredEVERY deed survey number inside the LP — one number off means uncovered
Plotted area + open spaceSellable extent + reserved 7.5% parks + 1% social infrastructureYour plot inside the plottable zone, not on reserved land
Mortgaged plotsThe 15% held as securityREJECT any mortgaged-list plot unless a release order is produced
Road widths + setbacksInternal roads min 12 m, master-plan frontagesWalk the layout — narrow roads and missing footpaths signal incomplete development
Land use + zoneMaster Plan 2031 designationNot in a Conservation Zone, FTL or lake/tank buffer
Sanction date + validityIssue date and conditionsOld LPs without infrastructure compliance can be REVOKED — ask for current status

Good sign: a digitally signed proceedings PDF with HMDA seal, an LP number that loads instantly, every survey number matching the deed, the mortgage list clearly marked.

The frauds this system catches

Fake LP numbers on impressive PDFs — the number simply does not exist on the public search: stop the deal; run BOTH the LP and file numbers through DPMS in your own presence. Phase-mixing — a genuine Phase 1 LP waved over Phase 2 plots the sanction never covered: match YOUR plot’s survey number to the approved extent, not the brochure’s. The mortgage blind spot — plots inside the 15% security sold before release: the DPMS search and the Ameerpet office both confirm release status; check before any advance.

Example: The FTL/buffer line in the table deserves its own sentence: Hyderabad’s costliest demolitions have been buildings inside lake buffers with paperwork that looked fine. The master-plan layer check is not a formality in this city — it is THE check.

Quick answers (FAQ)

DP vs layout approval? The DP is the broader sanction family (layouts, group housing, high-rises); the layout LP is its plotted-development face. Which portal now? BuildNow for everything fresh; DPMS for legacy checks. What releases the 15%? Physically built amenities + District Level BuildNow Committee certification. Can old LPs die? Yes — non-compliant ones can be revoked; always ask for current status.

Bottom line

One number anchors everything — but the professional read goes three layers deeper: tentative or final, your survey numbers inside the extent, your plot outside the mortgage and the buffers. That is the difference between buying a plot and buying a proceedings PDF.

For you: This is your own compliance playbook as you step into HMDA-belt development: budget the 15% mortgage into project cashflows from day one (it is trapped inventory until amenities finish — which is ALSO why finishing roads early pays twice), keep the deed-name-vs-BuildNow-registration identical, and hand buyers the DPMS search result for their exact plot. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 Building plan approval on BuildNow: the sanctioned plan, deemed approval, and the demolition task force

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Building Plan Approval Telangana 2026: GHMC & BuildNow Guide — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • The sanctioned plan is the urban local body’s permit that your design follows the master plan and the Telangana Municipalities Act 2019 — without it the building is illegal from day one.
  • It covers everything on the plot: setbacks, floors, height, parking, water/sewage, boundary walls — issued as a stamped drawing + proceedings letter.
  • From 15 Dec 2025, GHMC, HMDA and DTCP applications ALL route through BuildNow, with AI scrutiny checking drawings against zoning in minutes.
  • Plots up to 75 sq yards building G or G+1 need NO plan sanction — just online registration (₹1 token) and the fee with the first tax receipt.
  • If 21 days pass with no action, DEEMED APPROVAL kicks in under the TS-bPASS Act 2020.
  • The stick: every district’s Collector heads a Special Task Force that demolishes unauthorised buildings WITHOUT prior notice (s.174(4), TMA 2019).

What the sanctioned plan is

The permit for everything you intend to put on the plot. Legacy TG-bPASS sanctions on older buildings remain valid; every fresh application after the December 2025 cutover sits on BuildNow. The system’s design bargain: fast, AI-checked, self-certified approvals — matched by demolition powers that need no notice. Build inside the plan and the state is quick; build outside it and the state is quicker.

Applying online

1) Register on BuildNow — citizens, owners, builders and Licensed Technical Persons each get separate registrations; token ₹1. 2) Your LTP submits drawings, structural details, sale deed, link documents, EC, latest tax receipt and the layout’s LP number; the AI validates against the Building Rules in real time. 3) On a technically clean file, the fee letter arrives by SMS/email — scrutiny fees, development charges (G.O. 223), 1% labour cess, user charges — payable in 15 days. 4) Proceedings, sanctioned drawing and BP order land as PDFs; if 21 days pass without action, deemed approval applies. Keep a digital AND hard copy on site — inspectors ask.

Applying offline (panchayat areas)

Outside GHMC/HMDA/DTCP zones: the mandal or municipality office → application form + fee schedule → filled form, deed copy, tax receipt, layout approval, engineer-signed drawings, challan → the town-planning officer inspects (setbacks, road width, clearance from heritage, water bodies, high-tension lines) → Building Committee sanction → stamped plan + BP proceedings. Cross-check the file number on the notice board or the DTCP site to confirm authenticity.

Reading the sanctioned plan

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
BP / file numberPermit ID from BuildNow or GHMCPulls up the SAME plot on BuildNow Citizen Search
Owner + survey numberSanctioned holder and plotMatch deed and latest passbook entry
Plot area + built-up areaApproved extentsBuilt-up within the zone’s permissible FAR
SetbacksMandatory open space on all sidesWalk the site WITH A TAPE
Height + floorsThe sanctioned lineNo extra floor above it
Land use + zoneMaster-plan designationReject Conservation-Zone or unsanctioned-use buildings
Validity + sanction datePermit lifeExpired plans need revalidation before work restarts

Good sign: a BP number visible on Citizen Search, unbroken digital signature, drawings matching what stands on site, current validity.

The four problems buyers inherit

Built area exceeds the plan — the extra slab, the closed-in setback: even ~10% deviation invites penalty; have an engineer measure against the sanctioned drawing before buying, and demand any regularisation order. Plan doesn’t match the site — a real-looking stamp mapped to a DIFFERENT plot or older proposal: punch the BP number into Citizen Search; survey number and address must mirror the ground. Restricted-zone plots — Conservation Zones, tower-line and water-body buffers: pull the master-plan layer; a restricted shading means no genuine sanction exists. Sanction obtained by misrepresentation — revocable under the Act, and then the task force arrives; verify deed dates against the G.O. 251/261/265 cutoffs where relevant.

Example: For flat and villa buyers the lesson compresses to one motion: BP number into BuildNow Citizen Search, on your own phone, before the token advance. The seller’s PDF is a claim; the portal is the fact.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Do small plots need sanction? Up to 75 sq yd, G/G+1: registration only. What is deemed approval? 21 days of authority silence = approved, by law. Is a TG-bPASS sanction still valid? Yes — legacy sanctions stand; new filings go to BuildNow. Who demolishes? The Collector-led Special Task Force — without prior notice.

Bottom line

Telangana made legal building fast precisely so illegal building has no excuse. Verify the BP number yourself, measure the setbacks yourself — and never buy a deviation, however charming the extra floor.

For you: As you move from plots into construction, BuildNow discipline IS brand discipline: sanction before slab, zero deviation, plan displayed on site. And in acquisitions, price every deviated building as if the deviation must be regularised or removed — because under this Act, one inspection can make that literal overnight. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 NALA conversion (the Act & the process): from farmland classification to buildable land

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: NALA Land Conversion CLU Telangana Guide 2026 — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • NALA conversion — under the Telangana Agricultural Land (Conversion for Non-Agricultural Purposes) Act 2006, successor to the NALA Act 1963 — is the mandatory prior permission to change agricultural land to residential, commercial or industrial use.
  • Until the order exists, the land’s legal use is agriculture. Period.
  • The DEEMED-CONVERSION trap: build anything on unconverted farmland — even a shed or boundary wall — and the law treats it as converted, with a penalty of 50% of the conversion fee ON TOP of the fee itself.
  • Jurisdiction: RDO for most areas; HMDA handles CLU inside the metro region; DTCP outside — a DTCP order for HMDA-zone land may be worthless.
  • The order gates everything downstream: building permission, layout approval, plot registration.

What conversion is

Every parcel’s Pahani/RoR carries a classification, and agricultural classification means farming is the only permitted use — no house, no shop, no layout without changing it first. The process: apply to the competent authority (RDO / HMDA / DTCP by zone), a public notice is gazetted and objections invited, the NALA tax is paid, and the conversion order issues. Sellers in Hyderabad’s growth corridors have been presenting BUILT-ON agricultural plots without NALA orders — and the buyer inherits the liability. The two questions to ask early: is the land non-agricultural in the CURRENT Pahani? If not, where is the conversion order?

Definition — Deemed conversion: the law’s response to building first and asking later: unauthorised non-agricultural use is treated as a conversion that already happened — and billed as the full fee PLUS a 50% penalty, with Revenue Inspector notices and quarterly Collector reviews backing it.

Applying, step by step

1) The right portal: agricultural land in most of Telangana → the Dharani/Bhu Bharati “Application for NALA” flow or MeeSeva; HMDA-jurisdiction CLU → hmda.gov.in or TS-iPASS; DTCP for the rest. 2) Application + documents: Pattadar Passbook, Pahani/RoR 1-B, EC, survey sketch, Aadhaar, and the sale deed if recently purchased; the system pre-scrutinises and flags deficiencies; the public-notice/objections stage follows. 3) On preliminary approval a DEMAND NOTICE issues — pay the conversion tax within 30 days by challan; the rate depends on intended use (residential/commercial/industrial) and the locality’s population. 4) The conversion certificate issues in about a month. Then the step people skip: VERIFY the classification actually updated in the live record — order and record must be consistent; a mismatch means the update never processed.

The offline route

RDO for rural/semi-urban land; HMDA at Ameerpet for metro CLU; DTCP at AC Guards or district offices. Full document set upfront — partial submissions are sent back. A Revenue Inspector may field-inspect the current use before approval; be present or represented. Pay against the demand notice, collect the order, and take a CERTIFIED copy — the original is the single document gating every later building and layout approval. One more: an error in the order’s survey number or extent gets fixed at the issuing office IMMEDIATELY, before any construction — order errors cascade into every downstream stage.

Reading the conversion order

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
Survey number + extentThe parcel convertedEXACTLY matches passbook and Pahani — a discrepancy may mean the order does not cover the land being sold
Converted use typeResidential / commercial / industrialA residential order cannot host commercial construction
Issuing authorityRDO / HMDA / DTCPMust have jurisdiction over the plot — HMDA-zone land needs the HMDA/CLU order
Order dateWhen conversion was grantedConstruction BEFORE this date = deemed conversion with penalties
NALA tax detailsChallan number and amountVerifies the tax was actually paid

The buyer’s reading of all this

For anyone buying a “plot”, this Act is the difference between land and liability: an unconverted parcel priced as residential is farmland wearing a costume, and the conversion cost, timeline, penalty exposure and refusal risk all transfer to you at registration.

Example: The corridor classic: a “gated venture” near Shadnagar, compound wall up, roads graded — Pahani still says dry agricultural land. Everything visible was deemed conversion accruing penalty. The buyer who asked for the order number before the site visit spent nothing; the buyers who trusted the compound wall funded the developer’s NALA bill plus 50%.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Is NALA needed before building a house? Always, on agricultural land. What does it cost? A use- and locality-dependent rate on the land’s value — plus 50% penalty if you built first. Who approves in my zone? RDO / HMDA / DTCP by jurisdiction. Does the order allow building? It permits the USE — building permission and layout approval still follow separately.

Bottom line

Classification is permission. Get the order, verify the record updated, keep the certified copy — and treat any built-on-farmland bargain as the seller’s penalty invoice with your name being added to it.

For you: Your conversion sequencing is a margin lever: NALA cost is a percentage of VALUE, so converting early — before the corridor re-rates — is cheaper than converting late; but converting parcels you may resell raw wastes fee. Model it per parcel. And never let site works start a day before the order: the 50% penalty plus a Collector-review flag on your survey number is the most expensive impatience in this business. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).

📑 NALA on Bhu Bharati: the integrated flow, and what changes after the order

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: NALA Conversion in Telangana: Process, Fees and Timeline — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • NALA conversion applications now run through Bhu Bharati — the application, the record and the resulting classification in ONE system.
  • The classification on your Pahani is not a description, it is a PERMISSION — houses, plots, sheds and shops on wet/dry land need it changed first.
  • The fee is assessed as a percentage of the land’s VALUE; the deliverable is the conversion order — and the UPDATED RECORD behind it.
  • Conversion is parcel-specific and purpose-relevant: converting one survey number does not bless the neighbour, and NALA does not replace HMDA/DTCP/municipal permissions.
  • Integration quirk: parcels MID-conversion sometimes show “no records found” while modules sync — a known effect, not a deletion.

The application flow

1) Confirm the parcel QUALIFIES: clean record, no 22A listing, no assigned-land restrictions, identifiers verified — restricted land does not convert by application, at any fee. 2) File on Bhu Bharati with the passbook, record and parcel sketch — accuracy of extent and survey details matters. 3) Pay the assessed fee against the demand; keep the challan WITH the application number. 4) The Tahsildar or RDO passes conversion proceedings — then confirm the record’s classification actually updated: the updated record, not the order PDF, is the real deliverable.

What changes after conversion

AspectBefore (agricultural)After (converted)
Permitted useAgricultureResidential / commercial per the order
Record classificationWet / dryNon-agricultural
Registration pathAgri flow via Bhu BharatiNon-agri flow via IGRS
Bank lendingFarm-loan frameworksPlot and construction finance
Next approvals—HMDA / DTCP / municipal permissions
Market value basisPer-acre ratesPer-square-yard rates

Good sign: order in hand, Pahani reading non-agricultural, fee challan filed with the application number.

The four trouble patterns (three self-inflicted)

Built first, converting later — penalty and regularisation pain: stop works, seek conversion and regularisation, expect cost and delay. Record vanishes mid-conversion — module sync: wait, search by LPM, confirm at the MRO if prolonged. Fee assessment disputed — verify against the government value for the survey number and contest WITH evidence. Restricted land refused — assigned, endowment and 22A parcels do not convert; there is no application cure — the restriction itself must be lawfully resolved first.

Why the check precedes the price

A plot pitched for building is worth its price only if it can legally be built on. Unconverted agricultural land priced as plots is the oldest markup in the market — the buyer inheriting the conversion cost, timeline and risk. Ask ONE question early: is there a conversion order for this exact survey number? Then verify the classification on the LIVE record, not the brochure.

Example: Red flag, verbatim from the market: a venture selling “plots” on land whose Pahani still reads agricultural, with conversion “promised after booking”, is selling you their regulatory homework. The discount is not a discount.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Can I build a house on agricultural land? Not lawfully before conversion — penalties, no bank finance, no clean resale. Cost? A percentage of the land’s value; verify the base against the government value. Timeline? Documented, unrestricted parcels: weeks; disputes and thin documents stretch it. Does NALA replace HMDA/DTCP approval? Never — use first, planning permissions after. Land disappeared from search during NALA? Known sync effect — LPM search, a few days’ patience, MRO if it persists.

Bottom line

One system now carries the whole journey — which means one record answers the whole question. Order + updated classification + challan trail, verified on the live portal: that is what “converted” means, and nothing less does.

For you: The before/after table is your pricing calculator: conversion moves a parcel from per-acre to per-square-yard economics — which IS the plotting business in one row. Sequence it deliberately (convert when corridor value justifies the fee), and pre-empt the sync quirk by never scheduling registrations or bank valuations in the middle of a parcel’s NALA processing window. 📑 Property tax receipts & the PTIN: the small document that gates mutation

Telangana land document guide — an original Deccan Ledger lesson · source & further reading: Property Tax Receipt Telangana 2026: GHMC PTIN Guide & Download — 1acre.in

Key Highlights

  • The property tax receipt is the GHMC/municipal acknowledgment (GHMC Act 1955; TMA 2019) that the half-yearly tax is paid for a PTIN — doubling as ownership continuity proof and Nil-Dues confirmation.
  • Every property in GHMC’s 650 sq km (6 zones, 30 circles, 150 wards) carries a unique 10-digit PTIN (formerly 14).
  • Arrears run interest at 2% PER MONTH; a recent one-time settlement waived 90% of accumulated interest for owners clearing principal + 10% in a time-bound window.
  • Section 208 ties tax to MUTATION: the Deputy Commissioner approves mutation only after every paisa clears — including under-assessments found at inspection.
  • Banks and sub-registrars read the latest receipt and the dues line before clearing any transaction.

What the receipt shows

PTIN, owner name, door number, locality, plinth area, Monthly Rental Value (MRV), the half-year due, amount paid, and pending arrears. No PTIN match, no payment, no receipt. New constructions need a separate SELF-ASSESSMENT — a Tax Inspector visits, then the Deputy Commissioner allots the PTIN.

Definition — PTIN: the 10-digit Property Tax Identification Number — the property’s tax identity. Searchable by door number, owner name or registered mobile when unknown.

Paying and downloading (five minutes)

1) onlinepayments.ghmc.gov.in (municipalities outside GHMC: cdma.cgg.gov.in) — the same gateway covers property tax, vacant-land tax and trade licences. 2) Property Tax → Search Your Property Tax → PTIN or door number → the portal shows assessment, demand, receipt history and arrears WITH interest. 3) Pay by UPI/net banking/card against OTP to the registered mobile — a 5% Early Bird rebate applies to payments before 30 April. 4) Print Receipts → PTIN → save the PDF immediately. Banks and sub-registrars want the CURRENT and PREVIOUS half-year together — pull both before mutation or sale. Offline: MeeSeva/CSC/designated SBI branches; GHMC Bill Collectors’ PoS handhelds issue instant computerised receipts (never accept a manual chit); duplicates at the Circle Office; the written Nil-Dues confirmation from the Deputy Commissioner for mutation.

Reading the receipt

FieldWhat it meansWhat to check
PTINThe property’s tax IDMatches the seller’s earlier receipts and door number
Owner namePerson liableMatches the seller’s deed
Door number + localityAssessed addressMatches the house on site
Plinth area + MRVThe calculation baseCompare with the sanctioned plan — under-assessment is recovered later WITH penalty
Half-year demand + paidThe cycle’s duesFirst half due 31 July; second 15 October
Arrears + 2% interestThe hidden lineAny non-zero figure = Nil Dues not achieved
Receipt number + datePayment referenceRe-verify by punching the PTIN on the portal

Good sign: correct PTIN, owner matching the deed, both half-years cleared for current and prior years, zero under arrears.

The three traps

Tax still in the seller’s name years after sale — SRO auto-mutation covers post-June-2021 registrations; older purchases often still show the previous owner, complicating resale: file mutation under Section 208 with deed, latest receipt and the fee DD. Under-assessed plinth area — GIS drone mapping has flagged over a LAKH under-assessed properties; the small-area receipt’s arrears land on the NEXT owner: cross-check receipt plinth area against sanctioned plan and physical measurement before signing. Arrears hiding under “Outstanding” — the seller shows one clean half-year receipt while old dues compound at 2%/month (GHMC can attach the property under prolonged default): run the portal dues search YOURSELF.

Example: The buyer’s one-liner: the receipt the seller hands you is history; the DUES LINE on the portal is the present. Check the second before trusting the first.

Quick answers (FAQ)

Why does a land developer care about a building tax receipt? Because mutation — yours and your buyers’ — waits on Nil Dues; the small document gates the big one. Rebate? 5% Early Bird before 30 April. Lost receipts? Circle Office duplicates against PTIN + ID. New building? Self-assessment → inspection → PTIN.

Bottom line

₹ small, leverage large: dues cleared, both half-years in hand, plinth area honest — or the mutation queue is where the deal waits.

For you: Vacant-land tax runs on the same gateway — keep VLT current on every urban parcel you hold, because YOUR clean dues line is what lets your buyers mutate smoothly after registration. Add “portal dues check” (not receipt check) to the acquisition sheet for any built property, and the plinth-area-vs-plan comparison whenever a structure is included. Original Deccan Ledger guide lesson; full source guide on 1acre.in (links above).
